For the concrete estimator, the definitions of the full-data and observed risks, the identity \(\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})=\theta_\delta\{\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})\}\), and the surjectivity of \(\Gamma\) give \[\begin{aligned} &\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_n-\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\right| \\ &\qquad = \sup_{P\in\mathcal M} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_n-\theta_\delta(P)\right|. \end{aligned}\] Combining this equality with Theorem 1 and the choices \(c\le c_{\mathrm{risk}}\), \(C_{\mathrm{risk}}\le C\) yields \[c r \le R_{n,\mathrm F}^\star \le \sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_n-\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\right| \le C r .\]

  5. The same target transport gives the concrete coverage identity \[\inf_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n} \{\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\in \mathrm{CI}_n\} = \inf_{P\in\mathcal M} P^{\otimes n}\{\theta_\delta(P)\in \mathrm{CI}_n\}.\] The right-hand side is at least \(1-\alpha\) by Theorem 2, so \[\inf_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n} \{\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\in \mathrm{CI}_n\} \ge 1-\alpha .\]

  6. Expected length is transported by the same observed-margin argument. Since \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) is a function of the observed sample, its length depends on \(P^{\mathrm F}\) only through \(\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})\), and the surjectivity of \(\Gamma\) gives \[\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n) = \sup_{P\in\mathcal M} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n).\]

    For every observed law \(P\), the clipping in Algorithm 2 gives \[0\le \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n(z))\le 1.\] Thus the real expected-length bound from Theorem 2 gives \[\sup_{P\in\mathcal M} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n) \le C_{\mathrm{len}} r \le C r,\] where the last inequality uses \(C_{\mathrm{len}}\le C\) and \(r\ge0\). Combining this bound with the equality of the causal and observed expected-length criteria gives \[\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{\Gamma(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n) \le C r.\] The same inequality may be viewed in \(\mathbb R_{\ge0}\cup\{\infty\}\) through the order-preserving embedding of finite nonnegative real numbers; this is the extended order used below for the minimax length criterion.

  7. Finally, Lemma 14 makes \(\mathrm{CI}_n\) an admissible honest observed-sample interval, since \(n\ge4\), \(0<h\), \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\), and \(\delta+h\le1\). Hence, by the definition of \(L_n^\star\) and the expected-length bound just proved, \[L_{n,\mathrm F}^\star = L_n^\star \le \sup_{P\in\mathcal M} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_n) \le C r,\] with the two sides read in \(\mathbb R_{\ge0}\cup\{\infty\}\) by embedding the finite nonnegative quantities as finite extended values. The lower bound from Theorem 2, together with \(c\le c_{\mathrm{len}}\) and \(r\ge0\), gives, in the same extended order, \[c r \le c_{\mathrm{len}} r \le L_n^\star = L_{n,\mathrm F}^\star .\] Substituting back \(\delta=\delta_n\), \(h=h_n\), and \(r=r_n\) gives all displayed conclusions for every \(n\ge n_\star\).

∎
Proof of Proposition 4.

Write \(P\) for the observed margin of \(P^{\mathrm F}\), and write \(p_x=P(X=x)=P^{\mathrm F}(X=x)\). The hypotheses in Definition 27 and the design constants give \[J>0,\qquad \kappa\ge0,\qquad c_->0,\qquad c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+, \qquad 0<p_{\min}\le 1/J, \qquad 0<\bar\delta<1.\] In particular, \(p_x>0\) for every stratum, \(c_+\ge0\), and \(\bar\delta\le1\).

  1. Fix \(x\in\mathcal X\). Let \(\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P\) be the continuity-only regression extension selected in Definition 11; the observed-margin component of Definition 27 supplies the membership of \(P\) in \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\). For every measurable \(B\subseteq[0,\bar\delta]\), the full-data product identity from consistency, exchangeability, and the conditional-density law gives \[\int_B m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)\pi_x(a)\,da = p_x^{-1}\int_{\{X=x,\ A\in B\}}Y\,dP^{\mathrm F}.\] Indeed, under the normalized full-data law restricted to \(X=x\), the treatment coordinate has the conditional law with density \(\pi_x(a)\,da\) on \([0,1]\). Its latent-coordinate marginal is the pushforward \[\nu_x^{U} := \bigl((O,u)\mapsto u\bigr)_{\#} \left(p_x^{-1}\,P^{\mathrm F}\!\restriction\{(O,u):X(O)=x\}\right),\] equivalently, for every measurable \(C\subseteq\mathcal U\), \[\nu_x^{U}(C)=p_x^{-1}P^{\mathrm F}\{X=x,\ U\in C\}.\] With this normalization, Assumptions 6, 7, and 2 give the product factorization of the treatment and latent coordinates in stratum \(x\). Applying that factorization to \(\mathbf 1\{a\in B\}g_x(a,u)\), and using Definition 19, identifies the stratum integral with \[\int_B\left(\int g_x(a,u)\,d\nu_x^{U}(u)\right)\pi_x(a)\,da = \int_B m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)\pi_x(a)\,da .\] The observed-margin identity gives \[\int_{\{X=x,\ A\in B\}}Y\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \int_{\{X=x,\ A\in B\}}Y\,dP .\] The regression-version property of \(\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P\) in Definition 11, together with the conditional-density law, gives \[\int_{\{X=x,\ A\in B\}}Y\,dP = p_x\int_B \mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(a)\pi_x(a)\,da .\] Combining the three displays and using \(p_x>0\), \[\int_B m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)\pi_x(a)\,da = \int_B \mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(a)\pi_x(a)\,da \qquad (B\subseteq[0,\bar\delta]).\] The two products are integrable on \([0,\bar\delta]\): Definition 19, Assumption 8, and Definition 11 give continuity of the two mean functions on this compact interval, and Assumption 4 gives the envelope \(\pi_x(a)\le c_+a^\kappa\le c_+\) almost everywhere on \([0,\bar\delta]\). Equality of all measurable set integrals therefore gives \[m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)\pi_x(a) = \mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(a)\pi_x(a) \quad\text{for Lebesgue-a.e. }a\in[0,\bar\delta].\] For Lebesgue-a.e. \(a\in(0,\bar\delta]\), Assumption 4 also gives \[\pi_x(a)\ge c_-a^\kappa>0 .\] Since the endpoint \(0\) has Lebesgue measure zero, the two continuous functions \(m_x^{\mathrm F}\) and \(\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P\) agree Lebesgue-a.e. on \([0,\bar\delta]\). Applying the continuous-version uniqueness principle in Lemma 18, \[m_x^{\mathrm F}(a)=\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(a) \qquad \text{for every }a\in[0,\bar\delta].\]

  2. Fix \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\). Since \(\bar\delta\le1\), also \(\delta\in[0,1]\). On the full-data carrier define \[\varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{ret}}(\omega) = \begin{cases} Y(\omega),& \delta<A(\omega),\\ 0,& \delta\ge A(\omega), \end{cases} \qquad \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{atom}}(\omega) = \begin{cases} Y(\delta)(\omega),& A(\omega)\le\delta,\\ 0,& A(\omega)>\delta. \end{cases}\] The common consistency null set in Assumption 6, the observed-treatment support \(A\in[0,1]\) supplied by the observed-margin component of Definition 27 and transferred to the full-data law, and Definition 6 give \[Y\!\left(d_\delta(A)\right) = \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{ret}} + \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{atom}} \quad P^{\mathrm F}\text{-a.s.}\] On the branch \(\delta<A\), treatment support puts \(A\) in \([0,1]\), the clamp policy returns \(A\), and consistency identifies \(Y(A)\) with the observed outcome \(Y\). On the branch \(A\le\delta\), the clamp policy returns \(\delta\), so the displayed decomposition is the corresponding algebraic split. The retained summand is measurable because it is built from the observed outcome and the measurable threshold set \(\{A>\delta\}\). The atom summand is measurable because the potential-outcome process in Definition 27 is jointly measurable and \(\{A\le\delta\}\) is measurable. For integrability, the observed outcome support bounds \(\varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{ret}}\) by one almost surely, and Assumption 6 identifies \(Y(\delta)\) with the \([0,1]\)-valued structural response \(g_X(\delta,U)\) on the consistency event; hence \(\varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{atom}}\) is also bounded by one almost surely.

  3. The retained summand depends only on the observed coordinates. The observed-margin identity therefore gives \[\int \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{ret}}\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \int Y\,\mathbf 1\{A>\delta\}\,dP = \nu_\delta(P),\] where \(\nu_\delta(P)\) is defined in Definition 7.

  4. For the atom summand, decompose over strata and use the conditional product law for \(p_x^{-1}P^{\mathrm F}|_{\{X=x\}}\), now with the dose argument fixed at \(\delta\) and the treatment event \(A\le\delta\): \[\int \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{atom}}\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \sum_{x\in\mathcal X} p_x \left(\int_{[0,\delta]}\pi_x(a)\,da\right) m_x^{\mathrm F}(\delta).\] By Definition 7, \[q_x(\delta)=\int_{[0,\delta]}\pi_x(a)\,da,\] so \[\int \varphi_{\delta}^{\mathrm{atom}}\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \sum_{x\in\mathcal X}p_x q_x(\delta)m_x^{\mathrm F}(\delta).\]

  5. Combining the pathwise decomposition, integrability, and the two integral identities for the retained and atom summands, and using Definition 21, \[\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F}) = \nu_\delta(P) + \sum_{x\in\mathcal X}p_x q_x(\delta)m_x^{\mathrm F}(\delta).\] Applying the pointwise identity \(m_x^{\mathrm F}=\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P\) on \([0,\bar\delta]\) at \(a=\delta\) gives \[\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F}) = \nu_\delta(P) + \sum_{x\in\mathcal X}p_x q_x(\delta)\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta).\] The right-hand side is exactly \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\) by Definition 25. Since \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\) was arbitrary, the asserted equality holds throughout the declared threshold range.

∎
Proof of Proposition 5.

  1. The continuity-regime hypotheses give \[J>0,\quad 0\le \kappa,\quad 0<c_-,\quad c_-\le \kappa+1\le c_+,\quad 0<p_{\min}\le \frac1J,\quad 0<\bar\delta<1,\] and also \(0<\alpha<1/2\). The displayed inequalities are the continuity design constants needed in Proposition 4. The strict inequality \(0<p_{\min}\), together with Assumption 3, gives positive mass to every stratum of any \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\).

  2. Fix \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\). Put \[\mathsf d_{\mathrm{obs}}(o)=(X(o),A(o)),\qquad \nu=P\circ\mathsf d_{\mathrm{obs}}^{-1},\] and choose a measurable conditional-mean version \(\mu_x:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) whose restriction to \([0,\bar\delta]\) is the continuous extension \(\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P\) in Definition 11. Take a Borel regular conditional response kernel \[\mathsf K^0_{x,a}(\cdot)=\Pr_P\{Y\in\cdot\mid X=x,A=a\}\] for \(Y\) given \((X,A)\). Equivalently, for Borel \(H\subseteq\mathcal X\times\mathbb R\) and \(C\subseteq\mathbb R\), \[P\{\mathsf d_{\mathrm{obs}}(O)\in H,\ Y\in C\} = \int_H \mathsf K^0_{x,a}(C)\,\nu(d(x,a)).\] Let \[\operatorname{clip}(y)=\min\{\max\{y,0\},1\},\qquad \mathsf K^1_{x,a}=(\operatorname{clip})_{\#}\mathsf K^0_{x,a},\qquad \rho_P(x,a)=\int y\,\mathsf K^1_{x,a}(dy).\] Because \(Y\in[0,1]\) almost surely, the raw conditional kernel \(\mathsf K^0\) and its clipped pushforward \(\mathsf K^1\) agree for \(\nu\)-almost every \((x,a)\), so their conditional means agree there. The conditional-distribution formula for conditional expectation, combined with the defining conditional-expectation version in Definition 24, gives directly \[\rho_P(X,A)=\mu_X(A) \qquad P\text{-almost surely}.\] Define the total measurable response mean \[\eta_P(x,a)= \begin{cases} \operatorname{clip}\{\mu_x(a)\}, & a\in[0,\bar\delta],\\ \rho_P(x,a), & a\notin[0,\bar\delta]. \end{cases}\] Then \(\eta_P(x,a)\in[0,1]\) for every \((x,a)\), and \(a\mapsto\eta_P(x,a)\) is continuous on \([0,\bar\delta]\) for every \(x\). Moreover, \[\eta_P(X,A)=\rho_P(X,A)=\mathbb E_P[Y\mid X,A] \qquad P\text{-almost surely}.\] On the strip, the displayed almost-sure equality and \(\rho_P\in[0,1]\) show that clipping \(\mu_X(A)\) leaves the conditional mean unchanged. Off the strip, the equality is the definition of \(\eta_P\).

  3. Let \[\mathcal Z_\eta= \left\{(x,a):\int y\,\mathsf K^1_{x,a}(dy)\ne \eta_P(x,a)\right\}\] and define \[\mathsf K^\eta_{x,a} = \begin{cases} \delta_{\eta_P(x,a)}, & (x,a)\in\mathcal Z_\eta,\\ \mathsf K^1_{x,a}, & (x,a)\notin\mathcal Z_\eta . \end{cases}\] The almost-sure agreement of \(\eta_P\) with the outcome mean of \(\mathsf K^1\) gives \(\nu(\mathcal Z_\eta)=0\), so replacing \(\mathsf K^1\) by \(\mathsf K^\eta\) preserves the joint observed law. Also \[\int y\,\mathsf K^\eta_{x,a}(dy)=\eta_P(x,a), \qquad \mathsf K^\eta_{x,a}([0,1])=1\] for every \((x,a)\). With \(\lambda\) denoting Lebesgue measure on \([0,1]\), define \[\mathsf Q_x(a,u) = \sup\left\{s\in[0,1]:\mathsf K^\eta_{x,a}([0,s])<u\right\}, \qquad u\in[0,1].\] The strict-superlevel sets of this supremum are countable unions over rational thresholds, so \((x,a,u)\mapsto\mathsf Q_x(a,u)\) is jointly Borel measurable. The generalized-inverse identity on left rays gives \[(\mathsf Q_x(a,\cdot))_{\#}\lambda=\mathsf K^\eta_{x,a}.\] Construct \(P^{\mathrm F}\) on the latent carrier \(\mathcal U=[0,1]\) by first sampling \((x,a,u)\sim\nu\otimes\lambda\), taking the latent variable to be \(U=u\), and setting \[X=x,\qquad A=a,\qquad Y=\mathsf Q_x(a,u).\] Define the structural response maps and potential outcomes by \[g_x(b,u)=\mathsf Q_x(b,u),\qquad Y(b)=g_X(b,U)=\mathsf Q_X(b,U),\qquad b\in[0,1].\] The simultaneous definition gives Assumption 6. To verify Assumption 7, it suffices in this finite-stratum setting to check the bounded-Borel product-moment factorization used by the construction. For every stratum \(x\), every bounded Borel \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\), and every bounded Borel \(\chi:[0,1]\to\mathbb R\), let \(S_x=\{(x',a):x'=x\}\). The product law \(\nu\otimes\lambda\) gives \[\int_{\{X=x\}} \varphi(A)\chi(U)\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \left(\int_{S_x}\varphi(a)\,\nu(d(x',a))\right) \left(\int_0^1\chi(u)\,d\lambda(u)\right),\] \[\int_{\{X=x\}} \varphi(A)\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \int_{S_x}\varphi(a)\,\nu(d(x',a)), \qquad \int_{\{X=x\}} \chi(U)\,dP^{\mathrm F}=\nu(S_x)\int_0^1\chi(u)\,d\lambda(u),\] and \(P^{\mathrm F}(X=x)=\nu(S_x)\). Substitution yields \[P^{\mathrm F}(X=x) \int_{\{X=x\}} \varphi(A)\chi(U)\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \left(\int_{\{X=x\}} \varphi(A)\,dP^{\mathrm F}\right) \left(\int_{\{X=x\}} \chi(U)\,dP^{\mathrm F}\right).\] The observed margin is \(P\): the conditional law of \(Y\) given \((X,A)=(x,a)\) is \(\mathsf K^\eta_{x,a}\), this kernel equals \(\mathsf K^1\) for \(\nu\)-almost every \((x,a)\), and \(\mathsf K^1\) agrees \(\nu\)-almost surely with the original conditional response law because \(Y\in[0,1]\) \(P\)-almost surely. Finally, for every stratum \(x\) and every \(a\in[0,\bar\delta]\), Definition 19 and the quantile identity give \[m_x^{\mathrm F}(a) = P^{\mathrm F}(X=x)^{-1} \int_{\{X=x\}}\mathsf Q_x(a,U)\,dP^{\mathrm F} = \int_0^1 \mathsf Q_x(a,u)\,d\lambda(u) = \eta_P(x,a),\] where \(P^{\mathrm F}(X=x)>0\) follows from \(0<p_{\min}\) and Assumption 3. Since \(a\mapsto\eta_P(x,a)\) is continuous on \([0,\bar\delta]\), Assumption 8 holds. Therefore \[P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}, \qquad P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}=P,\] with the observed margin understood as in Definition 29. This proves surjectivity.

  4. Now fix \(n\in\mathbb N\) and \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\). For every \(P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\), the design constants recorded above allow Proposition 4 to identify the causal and observed continuity targets: \[\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F}) = \theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}).\] The observed product sample law in Definition 2 depends on \(P^{\mathrm F}\) only through the observed margin \(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}\). Hence, for every observed-sample estimator \(\widehat\theta\) satisfying the measurability and range requirements in Definition 28, \[\left\{ \int \left|\widehat\theta(z)-\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\right|\, d(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}(z): P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F} \right\} = \left\{ \int \left|\widehat\theta(z)-\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right|\, dP^{\otimes n}(z): P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}} \right\}.\] The inclusion from left to right uses \(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\); the inclusion from right to left uses surjectivity of the observed-margin map \(\Gamma\) onto \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\). Taking the supremum of equal value sets and then the same infimum over observed-sample estimators in Definition 28 yields \[R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star = R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] The argument also covers \(n=0\), since it uses only equality of the defining value sets.

  5. The interval criteria are handled in the same way. For every observed-sample interval procedure \(C_n\) satisfying the measurability requirement in Definition 28, the target identity and surjectivity give the equivalence \[\forall P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F},\quad 1-\alpha\le (P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n} \!\left\{z:\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F})\in C_n(z)\right\}\] if and only if \[\forall P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}},\quad 1-\alpha\le P^{\otimes n} \!\left\{z:\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in C_n(z)\right\}.\] Using the pointwise interval length from Definition 15, the same observed-margin argument gives equality of expected-length value sets: \[\left\{ \int \operatorname{len}\{C_n(z)\}\,d(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}(z): P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F} \right\} = \left\{ \int \operatorname{len}\{C_n(z)\}\,dP^{\otimes n}(z): P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}} \right\}.\] Taking the supremum over laws and then the infimum over honest interval procedures in Definition 28 yields \[L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star = L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] Together with the risk equality, this proves the two displayed identities for every \(n\in\mathbb N\) and every \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\).

∎
Proof of Theorem 5.

1. Lower experiments for the observed criteria. For \(n\geq1\) and a threshold sequence write \[r_{n,\mathrm{root}}=(n:\mathbb R)^{-1/2}, \qquad a_{n,\mathrm{atom}}=\delta_n^{\kappa+1}, \qquad s_n=r_{n,\mathrm{root}}+a_{n,\mathrm{atom}},\] as in Definition 26. The lower-bound witnesses all use the same design: \[\Pr(X=x)=\frac1J,\qquad \pi_x(a)=(\kappa+1)a^\kappa,\quad 0\leq a\leq1 .\] Since \(0<J\), \(0\leq\kappa\), \(0<p_{\min}\leq1/J\), and \(c_-\leq\kappa+1\leq c_+\), this design satisfies the stratum-mass and thinning requirements in Definition 24. For the root term, set \[Q_{\mathrm{root},0}(Y=1\mid X,A)=\frac12,\qquad Q_{\mathrm{root},1}(Y=1\mid X,A)=\frac12+\varepsilon_n, \qquad \varepsilon_n=\frac14 r_{n,\mathrm{root}} .\] The two continuous regressions lie in \([0,1]\), their targets differ by \[\left| \theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(Q_{\mathrm{root},1}) -\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(Q_{\mathrm{root},0}) \right| =\varepsilon_n,\] and the product chi-square divergence obeys \[1+\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{root},1}^{\otimes n},Q_{\mathrm{root},0}^{\otimes n}) \leq \exp\{4n\varepsilon_n^2\}=\exp(1/4).\] For the atom term, let \(\varphi:\mathbb R\to[0,1]\) be the fixed smooth treatment bump with \(\varphi(0)=1\) and \(\varphi(u)=0\) whenever \(|u|\geq1\). Put \(a_\star=1/4\) and choose \[A_\star=8(\kappa+1)a_\star^2(\bar\delta+1)^\kappa, \qquad K_{\mathrm{risk}}=1+A_\star, \qquad h_{n,\mathrm{risk}}=\frac1{K_{\mathrm{risk}}n}.\] With \[q_{n,\mathrm{risk}}(a)=a_\star\, \varphi\!\left(\frac{a-\delta_n}{h_{n,\mathrm{risk}}}\right),\] define \(Q_{\mathrm{atom},0}\) by regression \(1/2\) and \(Q_{\mathrm{atom},1}\) by regression \(1/2+q_{n,\mathrm{risk}}(a)\). The bump properties give a continuous perturbation with \(0\leq q_{n,\mathrm{risk}}\leq a_\star\), support contained in \([\delta_n-h_{n,\mathrm{risk}},\delta_n+h_{n,\mathrm{risk}}]\), and value \(q_{n,\mathrm{risk}}(\delta_n)=a_\star\). Therefore the atom contribution at the clamp point gives \[a_\star\,\delta_n^{\kappa+1} \leq \left| \theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(Q_{\mathrm{atom},1}) -\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(Q_{\mathrm{atom},0}) \right|.\] The same localization and boundedness give the design-integral estimate behind the Bernoulli chi-square calculation, \[\mathbb E\!\bigl[4q_{n,\mathrm{risk}}(A)^2\bigr] \leq 8(\kappa+1)a_\star^2h_{n,\mathrm{risk}} (\delta_n+h_{n,\mathrm{risk}})^\kappa,\] under the displayed common design. Since \(h_{n,\mathrm{risk}}\leq1\) and \(\delta_n\leq\bar\delta\), the exponent is at most \(A_\star/K_{\mathrm{risk}}\leq1\), and hence \[1+\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{atom},1}^{\otimes n},Q_{\mathrm{atom},0}^{\otimes n}) \leq \exp\!\left\{n\,8(\kappa+1)a_\star^2h_{n,\mathrm{risk}} (\delta_n+h_{n,\mathrm{risk}})^\kappa\right\} \leq e .\] We use the following two-point risk bound. For probability laws \(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1\) on the sample space and real target values \(\vartheta_0,\vartheta_1\), every estimator \(\mathfrak t\) satisfies \[\max_{\iota\in\{0,1\}} \mathbb E_{\mathfrak Q_\iota}|\mathfrak t-\vartheta_\iota| \geq \frac{|\vartheta_1-\vartheta_0|}{2} \{1-\operatorname{TV}(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1)\}.\] To verify this, fix a probability measure \(\Lambda\) dominating both laws and define the common-part measure \[\mathfrak Q_\wedge(E)= \int_E \min\left\{\frac{\mathrm d\mathfrak Q_0}{\mathrm d\Lambda}, \frac{\mathrm d\mathfrak Q_1}{\mathrm d\Lambda}\right\} \,\mathrm d\Lambda .\] Its total mass is \(1-\operatorname{TV}(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1)\), and integration of \(|\mathfrak t-\vartheta_0|+|\mathfrak t-\vartheta_1|\geq |\vartheta_1-\vartheta_0|\) against \(\mathfrak Q_\wedge\) gives the display. When \(\mathfrak Q_1\ll\mathfrak Q_0\), Cauchy–Schwarz applied to the likelihood ratio gives \[\operatorname{TV}(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1) \leq \frac12\sqrt{\chi^2(\mathfrak Q_1,\mathfrak Q_0)}.\] For the root pair, the product bound gives \(\chi^2\leq e^{1/4}-1<4\); for the atom pair it gives \(\chi^2\leq e-1<4\). Hence the factors \(1-\frac12\sqrt{\chi^2}\) are positive in both two-point applications. The two target separations therefore yield constants \(c_{\mathrm R,0},c_{\mathrm R,1}>0\) such that, eventually, \[c_{\mathrm R,0}r_{n,\mathrm{root}}\leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star, \qquad c_{\mathrm R,1}a_{n,\mathrm{atom}}\leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] Taking \(c_{\mathrm R}=\min\{c_{\mathrm R,0},c_{\mathrm R,1}\}/2\) gives \[c_{\mathrm R}s_n\leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] For honest length, set \[\eta_\alpha=1-2\alpha, \qquad \chi_\alpha=\eta_\alpha^2/16, \qquad \tau_\alpha=\eta_\alpha/16 .\] For the root length witnesses, let \(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},0}\) and \(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},1}\) be the global-shift pair with \(\varepsilon_{n,\mathrm{len}}=\tau_\alpha r_{n,\mathrm{root}}\). Then \[1+\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},1}^{\otimes n}, Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},0}^{\otimes n}) \leq \exp(\eta_\alpha^2/64), \qquad \chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},1}^{\otimes n}, Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{root},0}^{\otimes n})\leq \chi_\alpha .\] The second inequality follows from \(0<\eta_\alpha<1\), \(e^x\leq1+x+x^2\) at \(x=\eta_\alpha^2/64\), and \(x+x^2\leq\eta_\alpha^2/16\). For the atom length witnesses, let \(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{atom},0}\) and \(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{atom},1}\) be the localized-bump pair with the same amplitude \(a_\star\) and choose \[K_{\mathrm{len}}=\eta_\alpha^2+64A_\star, \qquad h_{n,\mathrm{len}}=\frac{\eta_\alpha^2}{K_{\mathrm{len}}n}.\] The localized-bump exponent satisfies \[n\,8(\kappa+1)a_\star^2h_{n,\mathrm{len}} (\delta_n+h_{n,\mathrm{len}})^\kappa \leq \frac{A_\star\eta_\alpha^2}{K_{\mathrm{len}}} \leq \frac{\eta_\alpha^2}{64},\] so the same exponential-to-polynomial calculation gives \[\chi^2(Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{atom},1}^{\otimes n}, Q_{\mathrm{len},\mathrm{atom},0}^{\otimes n})\leq \chi_\alpha.\] For either length-witness pair, write the first law as \(\mathfrak Q_0\), the second as \(\mathfrak Q_1\), and the two target values as \(\vartheta_0,\vartheta_1\). If an interval covers \(\vartheta_\iota\) under \(\mathfrak Q_\iota\) with probability at least \(1-\alpha\) for \(\iota=0,1\), then total variation and the union bound give \[\mathfrak Q_0\{\vartheta_0,\vartheta_1\in C_n\} \geq 1-2\alpha-\operatorname{TV}(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1).\] Together with \(\operatorname{TV}(\mathfrak Q_0,\mathfrak Q_1)\leq \frac12\sqrt{\chi^2(\mathfrak Q_1,\mathfrak Q_0)}\), the displayed chi-square bounds give \[1-2\alpha-\frac12\sqrt{\chi^2(\mathfrak Q_1,\mathfrak Q_0)} \geq \eta_\alpha-\frac12\sqrt{\chi_\alpha} = \frac78\eta_\alpha>0 .\] On the event \(\{\vartheta_0,\vartheta_1\in C_n\}\), the interval length is at least \(|\vartheta_1-\vartheta_0|\). Thus there are \(c_{\mathrm L,0},c_{\mathrm L,1}>0\) with \[c_{\mathrm L,0}r_{n,\mathrm{root}} \leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star, \qquad c_{\mathrm L,1}a_{n,\mathrm{atom}} \leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\] in the extended nonnegative-real order, where nonnegative real lower bounds are embedded as finite extended values. With \(c_{\mathrm L}=\min\{c_{\mathrm L,0},c_{\mathrm L,1}\}/2\), the combination is by the case split \(c_{\mathrm L,0}r_{n,\mathrm{root}}\leq c_{\mathrm L,1}a_{n,\mathrm{atom}}\) or its complement, yielding \[c_{\mathrm L}s_n\leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\]

2. Upper risk for the fixed fallback estimator. Fix \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\), a sample split \(B_n=(I_0,I_1,I_2)\), and \(\delta=\delta_n\). Define \[\widehat\nu_n = |I_0|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_0}Y_i\mathbf 1\{A_i>\delta\}, \qquad \widehat b_{n,x} = |I_1|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_1}\mathbf 1\{X_i=x,\ A_i\leq\delta\},\] \[\nu_\delta(P)=\mathbb E_P[Y\mathbf 1\{A>\delta\}], \qquad b_x(P,\delta)=p_xq_x(\delta), \qquad \mathfrak e_\delta=\frac{c_+\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}.\] The estimator in Algorithm 3 is \[\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}} = \Pi_{[0,1]}\!\left(\widehat\nu_n+\frac12\sum_x\widehat b_{n,x}\right),\] where \(\Pi_{[0,1]}(t)=\min\{1,\max\{0,t\}\}\). By Definition 25, the target atom contribution is \[A_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P) = \sum_x b_x(P,\delta)\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta), \qquad \theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)=\nu_\delta(P)+A_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P).\] Since \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in[0,1]\), projection is contractive toward the target. Adding and subtracting the fallback atom term gives \[\begin{aligned} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right| &\leq \left|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)\right| +\left|\frac12\sum_x\widehat b_{n,x} -\sum_x b_x(P,\delta)\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta)\right|\\ &\leq \left|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)\right| +\frac12\sum_x\left|\widehat b_{n,x}-b_x(P,\delta)\right| +\sum_x b_x(P,\delta)\left|\frac12-\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta)\right|\\ &\leq \left|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)\right| +\sum_x\left\{ \frac12\left|\widehat b_{n,x}-b_x(P,\delta)\right| +\frac12\mathfrak e_\delta \right\}, \end{aligned}\] Here \[0\leq b_x(P,\delta)=p_xq_x(\delta) \leq \frac{c_+\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1} =\mathfrak e_\delta,\] by the thinning envelope integrated over \([0,\delta]\). The retained and atom indicators are \([0,1]\)-valued block averages, so Cauchy–Schwarz and the variance bound \(\operatorname{Var}(V)\leq1/4\) for \(V\in[0,1]\) give \[\mathbb E_P\left|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)\right| \leq \frac12 |I_0|^{-1/2}, \qquad \mathbb E_P\left|\widehat b_{n,x}-b_x(P,\delta)\right| \leq \frac12 |I_1|^{-1/2}.\] Integrating the pointwise bound therefore gives the explicit estimate \[\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right| \leq \frac12 |I_0|^{-1/2} +J\left\{\frac14 |I_1|^{-1/2} +\frac12\frac{c_+\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}\right\}.\] For all \(n\geq8\), admissibility of \(B_n\) gives \(|I_j|^{-1/2}\leq3n^{-1/2}\) for \(j=0,1\). Hence, uniformly over \(P\), \[\sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right| \leq C_{\mathrm R}s_n\] for a constant \(C_{\mathrm R}>0\) depending only on the declared constants. The same estimator is an admissible competitor in Definition 28, so \[R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star \leq \sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right|.\]

3. Coverage and length of the continuity interval. Let \[t_{0,\alpha}=\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_0|}}, \qquad t_{1,\alpha}=\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_1|}}, \qquad \widehat B_n=\sum_x\widehat b_{n,x}.\] The interval in Algorithm 4 is \[\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}} = \left[ \max\{0,\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-(t_{0,\alpha}+t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n/2)\}, \, \min\{1,\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}+(t_{0,\alpha}+t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n/2)\} \right].\] The coverage argument uses the following unit-range specialization of (Hoeffding, 1963): an average of \(m\) independent \([0,1]\)-valued variables has two-sided deviation probability at radius \(t>0\) at most \(2\exp(-2mt^2)\). The total empirical atom mass is itself one such average, because the strata partition the sample space: \[\widehat B_n =\sum_x\widehat b_{n,x} =|I_1|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_1}\mathbf 1\{A_i\leq\delta\}.\] Its population mean is \[B_\delta(P)=\sum_x b_x(P,\delta)=P(A\leq\delta).\] Applied to the retained-course average and to this total lower-threshold atom average, with the radii in Algorithm 4, the Hoeffding bound gives \[P^{\otimes n}\!\left( \left|\widehat\nu_n-\nu_\delta(P)\right|>t_{0,\alpha} \right)\leq\frac\alpha2, \qquad P^{\otimes n}\!\left( \left|\widehat B_n-B_\delta(P)\right|>t_{1,\alpha} \right)\leq\frac\alpha2 .\] On the complement of the two events, set \[A_\delta(P)=\sum_x b_x(P,\delta)\mu_{x,\mathrm{cont}}^P(\delta).\] The regression values lie in \([0,1]\), so the unknown clamp atom contribution satisfies \[0\leq A_\delta(P)\leq B_\delta(P).\] Since the concentration event also gives \(B_\delta(P)\leq \widehat B_n+t_{1,\alpha}\), the quantity \(\widehat B_n/2-A_\delta(P)\) lies between \(-\widehat B_n/2-t_{1,\alpha}\) and \(\widehat B_n/2\). Hence \[\left|\frac12\widehat B_n-A_\delta(P)\right| \leq t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n/2.\] Using Definition 25 and the contraction of projection onto \([0,1]\) toward \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in[0,1]\), this gives \[\left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right| \leq t_{0,\alpha}+t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n/2,\] so \(\theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\). The union bound yields uniform coverage at least \(1-\alpha\). Also, \[\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}) \leq 2t_{0,\alpha}+2t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n .\] Moreover, the displayed identity for \(\widehat B_n\) gives \[\mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\widehat B_n =B_\delta(P) =\sum_x b_x(P,\delta) \leq J\mathfrak e_\delta .\] Write \([u]_{\overline{\mathbb R}_+}\) for the canonical embedding of a nonnegative real \(u\) into the extended nonnegative reals. Consequently, before the final uniform simplification one has the extended-real length bound \[\int^{+}\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}})\,dP^{\otimes n} \leq \left[ 2\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_0|}} +2\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_1|}} +J\frac{c_+\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1} \right]_{\overline{\mathbb R}_+}.\] Since \(|I_1|^{-1/2}\geq0\), this immediately implies the deliberately weaker bound \[\int^{+}\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}})\,dP^{\otimes n} \leq \left[ 2\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_0|}} +2\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\alpha)}{2|I_1|}} +J\left\{\frac12|I_1|^{-1/2} +\frac{c_+\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}\right\} \right]_{\overline{\mathbb R}_+}.\] The first display follows from the pointwise inequality \(\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}})\leq2t_{0,\alpha}+2t_{1,\alpha}+\widehat B_n\) and the preceding expectation calculation; the second only enlarges the finite real upper bound by a nonnegative term. With \(|I_j|^{-1/2}\leq3n^{-1/2}\) and \(K_\alpha=\sqrt{\log(4/\alpha)/2}\), this gives a constant \(C_{\mathrm L}>0\) such that \[\sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}}\operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}) \leq C_{\mathrm L}s_n\] and the same comparison holds in the extended nonnegative-real order after embedding the real bound as a finite extended value.

4. Transport to the causal continuity class. Let \(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}\) denote the observed margin in Definition 29. Since the estimator and interval are functions only of the observed coordinates, for every nonnegative or integrable observed-sample functional \(H\), \[\mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F})^{\otimes n}}H(O_1,\ldots,O_n) = \mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}}H(O_1,\ldots,O_n),\] and the analogous identity holds for observed-sample events. The causal minimax criteria in Definition 28 are defined with the full-data product law. For the observed-only estimator and interval used here, the preceding pushforward identity gives the corresponding fixed-procedure risk, coverage, and expected length as the same quantities computed under the observed-margin product law. By Proposition 4, every \(P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\) and every \(\delta\in[0,\bar\delta]\) satisfy \[\psi_\delta(P^{\mathrm F}) = \theta_{\delta,\mathrm{cont}}(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}}).\] By Proposition 5, every \(P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}\) is the observed margin of some member of \(\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}\), and the observed and causal criteria in Definition 28 agree exactly: \[R_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star=R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star, \qquad L_{n,\mathrm{cont},\mathrm F}^\star=L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] The bridge identity and observed-margin surjectivity also identify the fixed-procedure observed and causal worst-case quantities. For the fallback estimator, \[\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\right| = \sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right|.\] For the interval, \[\inf_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}} (P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n} \{\psi_{\delta_n}(P^{\mathrm F})\in\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\} = \inf_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} P^{\otimes n} \{\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\in\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}\},\] and the expected lengths satisfy \[\sup_{P^{\mathrm F}\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}^{\mathrm F}} \mathbb E_{(P^{\mathrm F}_{\mathrm{obs}})^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}) = \sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \operatorname{len}(\mathrm{CI}_{n,\mathrm{cont}}).\]

5. Choice of constants and eventual frontier bounds. Let \(c_{\mathrm R},c_{\mathrm L},C_{\mathrm R},C_{\mathrm L}\) be the constants obtained above and define \[c=\min\{c_{\mathrm R},c_{\mathrm L}\}, \qquad C=\max\{C_{\mathrm R},C_{\mathrm L}\}+c+1 .\] Then \(0<c<C\). Intersecting the eventual sets from the lower-risk, lower-length, upper-risk, and upper-length bounds with the sample-size threshold \(n\geq8\) gives, for every admissible threshold sequence and every admissible split sequence, all sufficiently large \(n\) satisfying; at those sample sizes the positivity of \(|I_0|\) and \(|I_1|\) used in the coverage and upper-bound arguments follows from split admissibility. \[c s_n\leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star \leq \sup_{P\in\mathcal M_{\mathrm{cont}}} \mathbb E_{P^{\otimes n}} \left|\widehat\theta_{n,\mathrm{cont}}-\theta_{\delta_n,\mathrm{cont}}(P)\right| \leq C s_n,\] the asserted observed coverage and length upper bounds, and, in the extended nonnegative-real order, \[c s_n\leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star.\] The equalities and causal bounds follow from the transport identities above with the same \(c\), \(C\), estimator, and interval.

6. Elbow, zero threshold, and fixed positive thresholds. Since \(\kappa+1>0\), for \(n>0\), \[\left(n^{-1/(2(\kappa+1))}\right)^{\kappa+1}=n^{-1/2},\] and therefore \[s_n\bigl(\delta_n=n^{-1/(2(\kappa+1))}\bigr)=2n^{-1/2}\asymp n^{-1/2}.\] At \(\delta_n=0\), the identity \(0^{\kappa+1}=0\) gives \[s_n(0)=n^{-1/2}\asymp n^{-1/2}.\] If \(\delta_n\to\delta_0\in(0,\bar\delta]\), then continuity of \(u\mapsto u^{\kappa+1}\) at the positive point \(\delta_0\) gives \[\delta_n^{\kappa+1}\to\delta_0^{\kappa+1}>0 .\] The fixed-positive-threshold argument applies the atom-scale lower constructions directly. For the risk criterion, there is a constant \(c_{\mathrm R,+}>0\) such that eventually \[c_{\mathrm R,+}\delta_n^{\kappa+1} \leq R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star .\] If \(R_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\to0\), passing to limits in this eventual inequality would give \(c_{\mathrm R,+}\delta_0^{\kappa+1}\leq0\), contradicting \(c_{\mathrm R,+}\delta_0^{\kappa+1}>0\). For honest length, the corresponding lower construction gives a constant \(c_{\mathrm L,+}>0\) with \[c_{\mathrm L,+}\delta_n^{\kappa+1} \leq L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\] eventually in the extended nonnegative-real order. Convergence of \(L_{n,\mathrm{cont}}^\star\) to zero would imply \(c_{\mathrm L,+}\delta_0^{\kappa+1}\leq0\), again impossible. Hence neither fixed-positive-threshold criterion tends to zero.

∎
Proof of Proposition 6.

Fix \(x\) and \(\delta\). By Definition 5, the conditional treatment law in stratum \(x\) is represented on \([0,1]\) by the density \(\pi_x\). By Definition 6, the clamp map is measurable and satisfies \[d_\delta(a)=\delta\quad\text{for }a\leq\delta, \qquad d_\delta(a)=a\quad\text{for }a>\delta .\] Splitting the conditional treatment law at \((-\infty,\delta]\) and its complement gives \[P\{d_\delta(A)\in\cdot\mid X=x\} = P\{A\in\cdot,\ A>\delta\mid X=x\} + P\{A\leq\delta\mid X=x\}\,\delta_\delta .\] Because this conditional treatment law is carried by \([0,1]\), the retained part is \[P\{A\in\cdot,\ A>\delta\mid X=x\} = P\{A\in\cdot,\ A\in(\delta,1]\mid X=x\}.\] The same density representation, together with \(0\leq\delta\leq\bar\delta<1\), gives \[P\{A\leq\delta\mid X=x\} = \int_{[0,\delta]}\pi_x(a)\,da = q_x(\delta),\] where the last equality is the definition of \(q_x(\delta)\) in Definition 7. These identities give the asserted pushforward decomposition.

It remains to bound \(q_x(\delta)\). The conditional-density and polynomial-thinning conditions in Definition 5 give integrability of \(\pi_x\) on \([0,\delta]\) and, for Lebesgue-a.e. \(a\in[0,\delta]\), \[c_-a^\kappa\leq \pi_x(a)\leq c_+a^\kappa .\] Since \(\kappa\geq0\), the function \(a\mapsto a^\kappa\) is integrable on \([0,\delta]\). Integrating the almost-everywhere inequalities yields \[c_-\int_0^\delta a^\kappa\,da \leq q_x(\delta) \leq c_+\int_0^\delta a^\kappa\,da .\] The regime condition \(\kappa\geq0\) implies \(-1<\kappa\), and with \(0\leq\delta\), \[\int_0^\delta a^\kappa\,da = \frac{\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1}.\] Substitution gives \[\frac{c_-\,\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1} \leq q_x(\delta) \leq \frac{c_+\,\delta^{\kappa+1}}{\kappa+1},\] as required.

∎
Proof of Proposition 7.

Fix the stratum \(x\), and let \[\mu_0=\operatorname{Leb}\!\restriction[0,1], \qquad \mu_x=\mu_0\text{ with density }\pi_x .\] Thus \(\mu_x\) is the conditional natural-treatment measure in stratum \(x\). The endpoints are \(\mu_0\)-null, so \[\mu_0\text{-almost every }a\text{ lies in }(0,1).\] By Assumption 4, for \(\mu_0\)-almost every \(a\in(0,1)\), \[\pi_x(a)\ge c_- a^\kappa>0,\] because \(c_->0\), \(\kappa\ge0\), and \(a^\kappa>0\) for \(a>0\). Hence \(\pi_x>0\) \(\mu_0\)-almost everywhere. Since \(\mu_x\) is obtained from \(\mu_0\) by this density, \(\mu_x\ll\mu_0\), and therefore \[\mu_x\text{-almost every }a\text{ lies in }(0,1).\] Fix such an \(a\), and put \[y=d_\delta(a)=\max\{a,\delta\},\] using Definition 6. Since \(0<a<1\), \(0\leq\delta\leq\bar\delta\), and \(\bar\delta<1\), we have \[0<y<1.\] Let \(U\subset\mathbb R\) be open with \(y\in U\). Choose \(\varepsilon>0\) such that \(B(y,\varepsilon)\subset U\), and set \[r=\min\{\varepsilon,y,1-y\}.\] Then \(r>0\) and \[B(y,r)\subset U\cap[0,1].\] Consequently, \[0<\operatorname{Leb}(B(y,r))\leq \mu_0(U).\] It remains to transfer this positivity from \(\mu_0\) to \(\mu_x\). For a measure with density, the zero-set identity gives, for every open \(E\), \[\mu_x(E)=0 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \mu_0\bigl(E\cap\{z:\pi_x(z)>0\}\bigr)=0 .\] If \(\mu_x(U)=0\), this identity gives \[\mu_0\bigl(U\cap\{z:\pi_x(z)>0\}\bigr)=0.\] Because \(\pi_x>0\) \(\mu_0\)-almost everywhere, the set in this display agrees with \(U\) up to a \(\mu_0\)-null set, contradicting \(0<\mu_0(U)\). Thus \(0<\mu_x(U)\). Since \(U\) was arbitrary, \(d_\delta(a)\) is a support point of the conditional natural-treatment measure in stratum \(x\). The choice of \(a\) was from a full \(\mu_x\)-measure set, which gives the claimed almost-everywhere support preservation.

∎
